d)[0] for d in unique_docs]\\n    \\n    point_auc = roc_auc_score(y, oof_proba)\\n    boot_aucs = np.empty(n_boot)\\n    \\n    for b in range(n_boot):\\n        sampled = rng.randint(0, n_docs, bootstrap_ci(\\n            y_pair, oof_proba, groups_pair, N_BOOT, rng\\n        )\\n        print(f\\\"  Point AUC (OOF): {point_auc:.4f}\\\", flush=True)\\n        print(f\\\"  95% CI: os\\nimport sys\\nfrom collections import Counter\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\n\\nimport numpy as np\\nimport torch\\nfrom scipy import stats\\nfrom tqdm import tqdm\\n\\nlogging.basicConfig(\\n    level=logging.INFO,\\n    format=\\\"%(asctime)s [%(levelname)s] %(message)s\\\",\\n    datefmt=\\\"%H:%M:%S\\\",\\n)\\nlog = logging.getLogger(__name__)\\n\\n\\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n\\ndef ngrams(tokens, n):\\n    return [tuple(tokens[i : i + n]) for i in range(len(tokens) - n + 1)]\\n\\n\\ndef simple_bleu(candidate_tokens, reference_tokens, max_n=4):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Compute sentence-level BLEU up to max_n grams with brevity penalty.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    if len(candidate_tokens) == 0:\\n        return 0.0\\n    precisions = []\\n    for n in range(1, max_n + 1):\\n        cand_ngrams = ngrams(candidate_tokens, n)\\n        ref_ngrams = ngrams(reference_tokens, n)\\n        if not cand_ngrams:\\n            precisions.append(0.0)\\n            continue\\n        ref_counts = Counter(ref_ngrams)\\n        clipped = 0\\n        cand_counts = Counter(cand_ngrams)\\n        for ng, cnt in cand_counts.items():\\n            clipped += min(cnt, ref_counts.get(ng, 0))\\n        precisions.append(clipped / len(cand_ngrams))\\n    if any(p == 0 for p in precisions):\\n        return 0.0\\n    log_avg = sum(math.log(p) for p in precisions) / max_n\\n    bp = min(1.0, math.exp(1 - len(reference_tokens) / max(len(candidate_tokens), 1)))\\n    return bp * math.exp(log_avg)\\n\\n\\n# ===================================================================\\n# Step 2: Seed data preparation\\n# ===================================================================\\n\\ndef prepare_seed_data(tokenizer, data_dir: Path, num_samples: int, max_tokens: int = 256):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Download C4 validation, truncate to max_tokens, save as depth_0.jsonl.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    out_path = data_dir / \\\"depth_0.jsonl\\\"\\n    if out_path.exists() and sum(1 for _ in open(out_path)) >= num_samples:\\n        log.info(\\\"depth_0.jsonl already exists with enough samples — skipping\\\")\\n        return\\n\\n    from datasets import load_dataset\\n\\n    # Use a single validation shard to avoid slow full-tree listing on proxied networks\\n    hf_endpoint = os.environ.get(\\\"HF_ENDPOINT\\\", \\\"https://huggingface.co\\\")\\n    C4_SHARD_URL = f\\\"{hf_endpoint}/datasets/allenai/c4/resolve/main/en/c4-validation.00000-of-00008.json.gz\\\"\\n as e:\\n        log.warning(f\\\"Direct shard download failed ({e}), falling back to full dataset listing\\\")\\n        ds = load_dataset(\\\"allenai/c4\\\", \\\"en\\\", split=\\\"validation\\\", streaming=True)\\n\\n    records = []\\n    for i, example in enumerate(tqdm(ds, total=num_samples, desc=\\\"Sampling C4\\\")):\\n        if i >= num_samples:\\n            break\\n        text = {"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"#!/usr/bin/env python3\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"7B Cross-Model Transfer Matrix.\\nTrain OBD-LightGBM on model A's features, test on model B's.\\nDiagonal: 5-fold GroupKFold CV. Off-diagonal: 100% train -> 100% test.\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nimport sys, json, argparse\\nimport numpy as np\\nimport pandas as pd\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\nfrom sklearn.model_selection import GroupKFold\\nfrom lightgbm import LGBMClassifier\\nfrom sklearn.metrics import roc_auc_score\\n\\ndef load_features(data_dir):\\n    df = pd.read_csv(Path(data_dir) / \\\"features.csv\\\")\\n    feature_cols = [c for c in df.columns if c not in ('doc_id', 'depth')]\\n    return df, feature_cols\\n\\ndef train_obd_predict(X_train, y_train, X_test, y_test, max_depth_val):\\n    results = {}\\n    for k in range(max_depth_val):\\n        d_lo, d_hi = k, k + 1\\n        mask_train = np.isin(y_train, [d_lo, d_hi])\\n        mask_test = np.isin(y_test, [d_lo, d_hi])\\n        if mask_train.sum() < 10 or mask_test.sum() < 10:\\n            continue\\n        yt = (y_train[mask_train] == d_hi).astype(int)\\n        yv = (y_test[mask_test] == d_hi).astype(int)\\n        clf = LGBMClassifier(n_estimators=200, max_depth=6, learning_rate=0.05,\\n                             num_leaves=31, verbose=-1, n_jobs=-1)\\n        clf.fit(X_train[mask_train], yt)\\n        pred = clf.predict_proba(X_test[mask_test])[:, 1]\\n        auc = roc_auc_score(yv, pred)\\n        results[f\\\"{d_lo}v{d_hi}\\\"] = auc\\n    return results\\n\\ndef run_self_transfer(df, feature_cols, n_folds=5):\\n    X = df[feature_cols].values\\n    y = df['depth'].values\\n    groups = df['doc_id'].values\\n    max_d = int(y.max())\\n    all_aucs = {f\\\"{k}v{k+1}\\\": [] for k in range(max_d)}\\n    gkf = GroupKFold(n_splits=n_folds)\\n    for train_idx, test_idx in gkf.split(X, y, groups):\\n        fold_aucs = train_obd_predict(X[train_idx], y[train_idx], X[test_idx], y[test_idx], max_d)\\n        for pair, auc in fold_aucs.items():\\n            all_aucs[pair].append(auc)\\n    return {pair: np.mean(aucs) for pair, aucs in all_aucs.items() if aucs}\\n\\ndef run_cross_transfer(df_train, df_test, feature_cols):\\n    X_tr = df_train[feature_cols].values\\n    y_tr = df_train['depth'].values\\n    X_te = df_test[feature_cols].values\\n    y_te = df_test['depth'].values\\n    max_d = min(int(y_tr.max()), int(y_te.max()))\\n    return train_obd_predict(X_tr, y_tr, X_te, y_te, max_d)\\n\\ndef main():\\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--data_dirs\\\", nargs=\\\"+\\\", required=True)\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.41}}"}

```text
{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"#!/usr/bin/env python3\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"Cross-model transfer heatmap: two-panel (ordinal accuracy + retention).\\n\\nInput:  embedded paper data or artifacts/*transfer*.json\\nOutput: artifacts/figures/fig3_transfer_heatmap.pdf\\nDeps:   matplotlib, numpy\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport argparse\\nimport json\\nimport matplotlib\\nmatplotlib.use(\\\"Agg\\\")\\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\\nimport numpy as np\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\n\\nMODELS = [\\\"Qwen-rw\\\", \\\"Pythia\\\", \\\"OLMo\\\", \\\"Qwen-cont\\\"]\\n\\nORDINAL_ACC = np.array([\\n    [0.423, 0.259, 0.265, 0.347],\\n    [0.272, 0.386, 0.353, 0.329],\\n    [0.277, 0.345, 0.382, 0.324],\\n    [0.344, 0.328, 0.311, 0.388],\\n])\\n\\n\\ndef compute_retention(acc):\\n    diag = np.diag(acc)\\n    return acc / diag[:, None] * 100\\n\\n\\ndef parse_args():\\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=\\\"Plot cross-model transfer heatmap\\\")\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--data-dir\\\", type=Path,\\n                        default=Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent)\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--output\\\", type=Path, default=None)\\n    return parser.parse_args()\\n\\n\\ndef main():\\n    args = parse_args()\\n    root = args.data_dir\\n\\n    acc = ORDINAL_ACC\\n    retention = compute_retention(acc)\\n    n = len(MODELS)\\n\\n    plt.rcParams.update({\\n        \\\"font.family\\\": \\\"serif\\\",\\n        \\\"font.size\\\": 11,\\n        \\\"axes.linewidth\\\": 0.8,\\n        \\\"xtick.major.width\\\": 0.8,\\n        \\\"ytick.major.width\\\": 0.8,\\n    })\\n\\n    fig, (ax1, ax2) = plt.subplots(1, 2, figsize=(10, 4.2), constrained_layout=True)\\n\\n    # --- Panel (a): Ordinal Accuracy ---\\n    cmap_acc = plt.cm.RdYlGn\\n    vmin_a, vmax_a = 0.25, max(0.43, np.nanmax(acc) + 0.01)\\n    im1 = ax1.imshow(acc, cmap=cmap_acc, vmin=vmin_a, vmax=vmax_a, aspect=\\\"equal\\\")\\n\\n    for i in range(n):\\n        for j in range(n):\\n            val = acc[i, j]\\n            color = \\\"white\\\" if val > (vmin_a + vmax_a) / 2 else \\\"black\\\"\\n            weight = \\\"bold\\\" if i == j else \\\"normal\\\"\\n            ax1.text(j, i, f\\\"{val:.3f}\\\", ha=\\\"center\\\", va=\\\"center\\\",\\n                     fontsize=10, color=color, fontweight=weight)\\n\\n    for i in range(n):\\n        rect = plt.Rectangle((i - 0.5, i - 0.5), 1, 1, linewidth=2.0,\\n                              edgecolor=\\\"#333333\\\", facecolor=\\\"none\\\", zorder=5)\\n        ax1.add_patch(rect)\\n\\n    ax1.set_xticks(range(n))\\n    ax1.set_xticklabels(MODELS, fontsize=9)\\n    ax1.set_yticks(range(n))\\n    ax1.set_yticklabels(MODELS, fontsize=9)\\n    ax1.set_xlabel(\\\"Test Model\\\", fontsize=10)\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.41}}"}
```

```text
Looking at the cross-scorer directories, the naming convention appears to be `scorer__generator`:
- `qwen_base__olmo` - scorer is qwen_base, generator is olmo
- `pythia_greedy__olmo` - scorer is pythia_greedy, generator is olmo

But for the main table OLMo results, the scorer would be the model itself (OLMo self-scoring). There's no directory for that.

Wait, let me look at the cross-scorer experiment more carefully. The 022 experiment tests cross-scorer configurations. But the original 5 C4 experiments (Table 1) must have used different data directories.
 
So the original pipeline had five configurations: Qwen-rewrite using rewrite mode, Qwen-cont and Pythia and OLMo and Qwen-7B all in continuation mode. Each one generates depth chain texts and then uses a scorer model to extract features, which could be the same model that generated the text or a different one.

Looking at the cross-scorer results, I can see combinations like Pythia scoring OLMo-generated text and Qwen-base scoring OLMo-generated text. But I'm realizing the original five experiments might have used self-scoring, and I need to figure out where those results are stored or if the data was removed. Let me reconsider whether these experiments might be in a different project directory or if I'm looking at the right features.csv files.
```

```text
But the main table OLMo uses self-scoring (OLMo scores its own output), giving 1v2 AUC = 0.6145. The depth chain texts (depth_0 to depth_5) are the same across all scorers since they're generated by OLMo regardless. Only the features change based on which model computes the surprisal.


To get OLMo self-scored features, I'd need to rerun the feature extraction with OLMo as the scorer on those same depth chains, which would be computationally expensive. But wait—I should double-check whether the main table actually uses self-scoring or if I'm misinterpreting the paper's setup.

Looking back at the paper, it mentions evaluating across multiple model configurations and notes that features are computed from token-level surprisal values using an "external scorer model"—so the scorer is a separate fixed model, not the generator itself. This suggests the main table likely uses a consistent scorer across all experiments, probably Qwen2.5-1.5B-base. But when I check the cross-scorer result for `qwen_base__olmo`, I get 0.6047 AUC, which doesn't match the main table's 0.6145. Maybe the main table uses Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct as the scorer instead?

Actually, rereading more carefully, the paper states that the default approach uses self-scoring, and the cross-scorer experiments are testing alternatives. So for the Qwen models in the main table, the self-scorer would be Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct, and for Pythia it would be Pythia-1.4B. The dramatic difference in results when switching scorers—like Pythia giving 0.8811 AUC versus Qwen's lower scores—shows how sensitive the discriminability is to which model does the scoring.

Now I'm trying to figure out the naming convention for these cross-scorer experiments. The notation `pythia_greedy__olmo` is ambiguous—it could mean either Pythia as the generator with OLMo scoring, or OLMo as the generator with Pythia scoring. The much higher AUC (0.8811) compared to the main table's 0.6145 suggests I need to clarify which model is actually generating the text being evaluated.
```

```text
The ROADMAP doesn't directly address the experiment numbering for OLMo-1B C4 or where its data is. Let me search the project's local report files or the task context for more clues.

Actually, let me take a step back. The task says "OLMo-1B C4 cont（当前 ci_lower=0.6045, margin +0.45pp）". The margin is 0.6045 - 0.6000 = 0.0045 = 0.45pp. This matches. And looking at the `qwen_base__olmo` cross-scorer result, the 1v2 AUC is 0.6047. 

A bootstrap CI lower bound that tight to the point estimate suggests either a very narrow confidence interval or just a thousand resamples. The task mentions "exp-002" as the OLMo experiment, but I'm not seeing that directory naming convention in the actual structure. The paper references "exp-018" for LLaMA-3.1-8B, so these experiment numbers might be internal identifiers that don't correspond directly to the folder names I'm looking at.

Let me focus on what matters: finding where the OLMo C4 features data lives for this bootstrap calculation. The evidence points to the `qwen_base__olmo` configuration—it has OLMo-generated C4 texts scored with Qwen-base features, yielding a 1v2 AUC of 0.6047. That's essentially identical to the ci_lower of 0.6045 mentioned in the task, with the 0.45 percentage point margin matching the gap from 0.6000. A thousand-sample bootstrap producing a lower bound this close to the point estimate makes sense for a relatively stable metric like this.

Actually, that logic breaks down—the ci_lower should sit below the point estimate, not nearly equal to it. If the point AUC is 0.6047, a ci_lower of 0.6045 implies almost no variance, which doesn't work. The plot script mentions that CIs come from bootstrap where available, but can be estimated as ±0.010 or ±0.002 for certain comparisons. If the OLMo self-scored 1v2 AUC is actually 0.6145 and the CI was estimated at ±0.010, then 0.6145 - 0.010 = 0.6045—that's the missing piece.

So the task is asking me to compute the actual bootstrap CI for the self-scored OLMo configuration, but I don't have those features readily available. I need to either re-extract them from the model or search more carefully for where they might already exist.
```
